## engineering (tech-lead + frontend + qa)
- summary: carried over from 2026-09-23 — FR-68/69/71/72/73/75/76 all `done` (full detail in `LOG.md`); FR-77/78/79 opened as findings from that day's work, planned into today. 09:00 shift 2026-09-24 (frontend) took FR-77 per today's plan: root cause traced in `frontend/src/App.tsx` — the `privacy-line` footer paragraph was already rendered directly below the ~100px `.skeleton` while loading, so when the API resolved and the ~10,000px `CallSheetView` swapped in for the skeleton in the same frame, that already-visible paragraph jumped ~10,000px down, matching FR-71's `main#main` culprit exactly. Fix: `privacy-line` now renders only once `!loading` (one line), so nothing pre-existing sits below the skeleton to be displaced. Re-ran `frontend/scripts/lighthouse_pilot_scale.py` before and after: Performance 69 → 94, Cumulative Layout Shift 0.843 → 0.014 (audit score 0.04 → 1.00); `cumulative-layout-shift`/`cls-culprits-insight` no longer red. Recorded in `docs/research/pilot-scale-2026-09.md` (before/after table), full report committed. Failure case checked: a nonexistent-id load still shows the not-found message with no shift reintroduced. PR #194 (`fix/fr-77-skeleton-layout-shift`), auto-merge enabled. FR-77 set to `done`.
  10:00 shift (tech-lead) took FR-78 per today's plan: `app/routers/call_sheets.py`'s `create_call_sheet_endpoint`/`publish_revision_endpoint`/`cancel_call_sheet` now queue delivery via FastAPI `BackgroundTasks` instead of awaiting `notify_subscribers`/`notify_day_cancelled` (FR-72's own measured ~7.7s at 50 subscribers); `app/services/notifications.py` gains `notify_subscribers_in_background`/`notify_day_cancelled_in_background`, each opening its own DB session since a background task can outlive the request's own. Picked the dod's "drop the response field" option: the publish/cancel response no longer carries a `notifications` summary (nothing is known synchronously any more); it now shows up on the existing FR-50 history panel instead (`build_history` reads the same `DistributionLog` rows, no new table). Documented caveat: a cancellation logs against the *current* revision too (it never creates a new one), so the count is cumulative for that revision, not scoped to one publish. Proved the actual decoupling directly in `backend/tests/test_push_scale.py::test_endpoint_returns_before_the_background_task_runs` by calling the router function with a real `BackgroundTasks()` and inspecting it, unexecuted, right after return — `TestClient` itself can't show this (it drives the whole ASGI request, background task included, to completion inside one blocking call, confirmed by reading `httpx.ASGITransport`'s source). `backend/tests/conftest.py` gains a `background_tasks_share_db` fixture so tests can see what a background task committed. Closed ADR-007's own "revisit if publish latency becomes noticeable" trigger. PR #196 (`fix/fr-78-background-push-delivery`), auto-merge enabled. FR-78 set to `done`. Backend changed: deploy needed (no migration).

Reproduced the exact axe algorithm first, in an isolated Playwright probe against `axe-core` directly with hand-built HTML variants, before touching any product code — confirmed two distinct root causes rather than guessing: `#langToggle`'s `aria-label` held a translated *action* sentence that didn't literally contain the printed *target-language word* (worst on the Czech direction: nominative "Čeština" vs. genitive "do češtiny"); `.contact-btn.copy`'s ⧉ icon was a real DOM text node — `aria-hidden` correctly excludes it from the accessible name, but axe compares against the control's *visible* text, which does include it, so the icon character itself was the mismatch (confirmed: putting the same glyph inside `aria-label` still failed once whitespace didn't line up exactly). Fixed both: dropped `aria-label` on the language toggle (both `App.tsx` and `EditorApp.tsx` have their own copy) in favour of the printed word itself, moving the old sentence to `title`; moved the copy icon to a CSS `::before` in the one shared `ContactCopyButton` component (13 render sites on the pilot-scale demo), keeping its existing `aria-label` (already carries the contact's name for context) unchanged. `docs/research/a11y-2026-09.md` gets the dated write-up. Re-ran `frontend/scripts/lighthouse_pilot_scale.py` against the rebuilt `dist/`: `label-content-name-mismatch` 0 → 1 (14 → 0 failing elements).   16:00 shift 2026-09-25 (qa) took FR-92 per today's plan — the first undone plan item (`gh pr list --state open` was empty at shift start, nothing skipped; git synced cleanly to `main`, 40 commits ahead of this session's checkout). `frontend/scripts/lighthouse_landing.py` (new) scores `landing.html`'s built output the way `lighthouse_pilot_scale.py` scores the public sheet, minus the API/DB seeding (the landing page is fully static). First run: Performance 97, Accessibility 100, Best Practices 92, SEO 100 — all above the dod's 90 line, but `cumulative-layout-shift` was orange (0.89) and Lighthouse's own `cls-culprits-insight` named the three `.shots` screenshot `<img>` elements as the cause. Root cause, checked against the real files rather than guessed: every landing screenshot (`frontend/scripts/take_landing_screenshots.py`) is a crop of a real DOM region at whatever height it actually rendered, but `landing.html` hardcoded a single guessed `width="390" height="220"` on all six `<img>` tags regardless — real sizes ranged from 390×388 to 335×881 (`.shots img { width:100%; height:auto }` in `landing.css` derives its reserved aspect-ratio box from these attributes, so the mismatch reflowed the whole page on load). Fixed at the source rather than just patching the numbers: `take_landing_screenshots.py` now records each screenshot's actual captured size and rewrites the matching `<img>` attributes in `landing.html` after every run, so a future regeneration (after any visual change to the sheet, per the script's own usage note) can't drift back into this bug. Also tried and reverted a `device_scale_factor=2` bump on the screenshot capture — it closed a separate, minor `image-size-responsive` low-resolution Best Practices finding (96→100) but roughly doubled every screenshot's file size and dropped Performance from 97 to 88 (a failing LCP), a worse regression than the one-point gain; left as a known, accepted gap per the dod's own failure case rather than shipped. Re-run after the width/height fix: Performance 97, Accessibility 100, Best Practices 96, SEO 100; `cumulative-layout-shift` no longer orange (0.91, CLS 0.094). The two remaining Lighthouse "red" line items (`lcp-discovery-insight`, `network-dependency-tree-insight`) are confirmed `weight: 0` diagnostic Insights, not scored audits — checked directly in the report JSON, not assumed. Full write-up in `docs/research/pilot-scale-2026-09.md`; raw report `docs/research/lighthouse-landing.json`. PR #232 (`feat/fr-92-lighthouse-landing`), auto-merge enabled, subscribed. Design choice stated plainly since the dod's own wording reads as zero-tolerance and this doc's own history doesn't support that literally for Performance: Accessibility/Best Practices/SEO/PDF page counts get zero tolerance (real code changes only, per this doc's history); Performance gets a 10-point tolerance band, since the FR-77/80/84/97 notes above document 88→97 swings with zero code change (CI-machine timing noise) — a literal zero-tolerance gate would fail most PRs on noise and train people to ignore it; CLS is instead gated on Lighthouse's own fixed 0.1 "good" line so a real regression is still caught regardless of Performance noise. PDF page counts never auto-update the baseline (a page count is deterministic, not a score); a legitimate change needs a human to edit `ci-baseline.json` in the same PR. Also fixed a real bug that would have made this permanently red on a real runner: all three scripts hardcoded `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` (this dev sandbox's own pre-installed Chromium, absent on a real GitHub Actions runner); each now falls back to Playwright's own resolved `executable_path` (wherever `ci.yml`'s existing `playwright install --with-deps chromium` step put it) when that sandbox path doesn't exist. **Flagged, not silently assumed**: this fallback could only be exercised locally by simulating the sandbox path's absence, since this sandbox always has it — the real-runner path is unverified until this PR's own CI run reports it; subscribed to the PR to watch for that. Fixed at the source: replaced the hand-rolled listener with SQLAlchemy 2.0's own built-in `join_transaction_mode="create_savepoint"` on the sessionmaker — the engine's maintained replacement for exactly this "join a Session into an external transaction for a test suite" recipe. Verified the dod's own failure case: reverted to the old fixture code, reconfirmed the warning reproduces every time (and fails outright under `-W error::sqlalchemy.exc.SAWarning`), then reapplied the fix and reconfirmed 10/10 full-suite runs clean under that same strict check, plus a normal `pytest -q` run (153/153, matching `ci.yml`'s own invocation, no CI config change needed). `backend/tests/conftest.py` only — no application code touched. Read `frontend/src/loader.ts` and `vite.config.ts` before writing anything: the service worker's call-sheet runtime cache is already `NetworkFirst`, so every `fetch()` tries the network first and would surface a fresh revision — the backlog item's own "service-worker update detection" framing named the wrong layer. The real gap was in `App.tsx`: `loadCallSheet()` was only ever called once, in a mount effect, so a tab left open from before a publish had nothing left to re-run that fetch and would show the stale revision forever, independent of the service worker. Fixed with a `visibilitychange` listener (re-fetches the instant the tab becomes visible again — the "picked the phone back up" case) plus a 60s periodic poll as a backstop for a screen left on continuously, mirroring `DayEditor.tsx`'s existing 60s read-receipt refresh interval (same pattern, not a new one). New Playwright test `test_open_personal_link_tab_picks_up_a_published_revision_without_a_manual_reload`: publishes REV B from a second tab while a personal-link viewer tab sits untouched, asserts it is still REV A (the regression this guards), then dispatches a `visibilitychange` event directly — headless Chromium in this sandbox does not reliably flip cross-tab `document.visibilityState` via `bring_to_front()`, confirmed by trying it first, so the test exercises the listener itself rather than the OS behaviour around it — and asserts REV B and the FR-23 "REV B replaces A" banner both appear. Verified the dod's own failure case for real: temporarily reverted the `App.tsx` fix, confirmed the new test fails loudly (tab stuck on REV A's `06:00`), restored it, confirmed it passes. Found and fixed six pairs with new hex values computed from the actual WCAG relative-luminance formula (not eyeballed): `--muted` (both `styles.css` 4.60:1→7.23:1 against `--paper-2`, and `landing.css`'s own separate token 5.02:1→7.89:1 against `--paper`), `--ink-2` (5.96:1→7.18:1 against `--rule`, the `.avatar-fallback` chip), `--ok-ink` (6.78:1→7.16:1, the online-status pill), `.tomorrow[data-kind="cancelled"] strong` (5.95:1→7.18:1, hardcoded hex, not a token), `.contact-btn.wa` (5.44:1→7.20:1 against its own white chip background), and `.quiet-link.whatsapp` (6.55:1→7.21:1) — this last one is the same WhatsApp-brand green as `.contact-btn.wa` but needed a *different* new value: `.quiet-link` resets to a transparent background (`styles.css`), so in the editor's Crew-tab "send link" row it renders directly on `--paper`, not on a white chip like the public sheet's version; the debug harness's own `bgColor: "#f7f4ee"` reading caught this live, a manual `:root`-only review would have missed it. Every other token (`--ink`, `--warn-ink`, `--err-ink`, the five `--rev-*` revision-paper colors) was already ≥7:1 against every background it's used on, confirmed by direct computation, and left untouched; no pair hit the item's own failure case.   17:11 shift 2026-10-12 (qa, the documented 17:00 Prague slot, last of the day) took FR-162 — confirming the six "enabled, clean" axe-core rules FR-157 left as CI regression gates actually fire on a real violation, the same unverified-gate shape FR-127 found once already with `label-content-name-mismatch`. Set up the environment from scratch (none of it was running at shift start): `service postgresql start`, created the `filmrunner`/`filmrunner_test` role and database, a fresh `backend/.venv` with `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm ci && npm run build` in `frontend/`. For each of the six rules, read the installed `axe-core` (4.13.x per its own `helpUrl`) source for that rule's `evaluate`/`after`/`matches` functions directly rather than guessing from the rule name, constructed the markup that source says should violate it, and injected it live via `page.evaluate` onto this suite's own demo call-sheet screen (`frontend_url + "/index.html?demo=1"`, a real build) in a temporary addition to `frontend/tests/test_a11y.py`, running the exact `audit()` rule config each time. Five fired clean: `css-orientation-lock` (an orientation `@media` query with a `transform: rotate`), `focus-order-semantics` (`<div role="listitem" tabindex="0">`), `p-as-heading` (a bold, enlarged `<p>` with no trailing period), `target-size` (two adjacent 10×10px buttons with no spacing — a single undersized button with ≥24px clearance correctly passes under WCAG 2.5.8's own spacing exception, tried first and ruled out as a false negative, not a gap), and `td-has-header` (a 4×4 table with no `<th>`). The sixth, `identical-links-same-purpose`, did not fire as a violation — investigated why rather than accepting silence at face value: the rule's `evaluate` always returns `true`, and its `after()` function can only ever downgrade a same-name/different-URL pair's result to `undefined` ("incomplete"), never to `false`. Confirmed directly against the real two-link case (`<a href="/fr162-a">Open</a><a href="/fr162-b">Open</a>`): reports `incomplete`, never `violations`, under `resultTypes: ['violations']` — **this rule is structurally incapable of ever failing this suite**, independent of what markup exists on any screen; the FR-157 table's "kept on as a regression gate" claim for it was never true. Reverted the temporary probe code immediately after recording each result — confirmed clean by `git diff` showing zero change to `test_a11y.py`, and the full baseline suite re-run clean (7/7) both before and after. `docs/research/axe-ci-gate-2026-10.md`'s rule table and prose updated with the confirmed result per rule and the root-cause finding for the sixth, exactly as the item's own dod and failure case ask — no app code changed, since the gap was in what the documentation claimed a rule did, not in the app's own markup. PR #466 (`test/fr-162-confirm-axe-rules-fire`), `docs/research/**` + `docs/company/BACKLOG.md` only, on TEAM.md's own auto-merge allowlist, auto-merge enabled, subscribed. FR-162 set to `done` — the PR is the whole deliverable. No backend change, no migration, no deploy needed.

No `approved` INBOX item to execute (#1/#3/#6/#7/#8 still `pending`; #2/#4/#5 already `done`). `list_pull_requests --state open` showed thirteen PRs (#346/#348/#350/#352/#354/#360/#366/#368/#374/#378/#382/#386/#388), none naming a dependency in FR-142's own dod, nothing skipped. Read `app/services/call_sheets.py`'s `publish_revision` and `app/routers/call_sheets.py`'s publish endpoint directly first: the only existing guard against a double-tap is the partial unique index `uq_call_sheet_revisions_one_current` (FR-69) — enough to stop two *committed* current revisions existing at once, but a genuine race (two overlapping requests) still either (a) serializes by accident via the row-lock on the superseded revision's own `is_current=False` update, after which the loser's own `INSERT` hits that unique index and raises an unhandled `IntegrityError` (500, not a clean idempotent response), or (b) when there's no prior current revision to lock on, the same unique-index race can fire with no serialization at all first. Neither path collapses cleanly into "one revision, one push" — confirmed this is the real gap by reproducing it with the lock removed (below), not assumed from reading the code alone. Fix: `publish_revision` now takes a Postgres transaction-scoped advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, keyed via `hashtext` on the call sheet id — `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` first, to skip the extra round-trip when nobody's contending) before doing any work; the call that has to wait re-reads the call sheet once it gets the lock, and if a revision was added while it waited, returns that revision with `is_new=False` instead of publishing a second one. `publish_revision_endpoint` only schedules the background push when `is_new` is true. A normal sequential pair of publishes (no contention) is unaffected — confirmed with a dedicated test, each still gets its own distinct revision. Verified the dod's own failure case live, not just described: temporarily deleted the lock block, reran the new concurrency test, it failed loudly with exactly the `IntegrityError`/`uq_call_sheet_revisions_one_current` race described above; reverted (`git diff`/`git status` confirmed byte-identical) before committing anything. New `backend/tests/test_publish_idempotency.py` uses two genuinely independent, real Postgres sessions/connections racing in two threads (the suite's shared, savepoint-wrapped `db_session`/`client` fixtures pin every request in a test to one connection and so can never race against themselves) — asserts exactly one new revision persists and exactly one push is attempted for the one subscribed crew member, plus a second test confirming the lock doesn't change the normal sequential-publish case. `python scripts/check_company_docs.py docs/company` clean.
